Another viewpoint: Let Q(z)=P(z)^2. We need coeff q_k≤T, sum1, and Q is a square polynomial with nonnegative square root coefficients. The set of squares of polynomials is a low-dimensional variety; find one with bounded coefficients. This is like finding a polynomial with nonnegative coefficients and bounded square coefficients. It might be related to **Golomb rulers/Costas arrays**: A 0/1 sequence whose self-convolution isapprox flat with 0/1? For 0/1, Q coefficients are counts of pair sums. - `self_conv(p)`: returns q length 2n-1. If p.ndim shape. For search, maybe use real FFT. Need be careful: `np.fft.irfft` can have tiny negative values; clip? For objective max, tiny negative fine. Direct np.convolve exact positive.
Implementation:
```
def self_convolution(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n <= 512:
       return np.convolve(a,a)
    size=2*n-1
    L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    fc=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:size]
    # due to FFT error, maybe clamp small neg? 
    return fc
```
For gradient crosscorr weights/p:
```
def crossconv_weights_p(w,p): # returns g_i = sum_j w_{i+j}p_j
    n=len(p)
    if n<=512:
       c=np.convolve(w,p[::-1])
       return c[n-1:2*n-1]
    size_needed = len(w)+len(p)-1
    L=1 << (size_needed-1).bit_length()
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fw*fp,L)[:size_needed]
    return c[n-1:2*n-1]
```
Check slice: full length len(w)+n-1 =3n-2. c[n-1:n+n-1]=c[n-1:2n-1] exclusive end, length n. Good.

- `smooth_max_and_grad(q, beta)`: q length 2n-1. Use stable. If beta <=0? maybe use exact max with subgradient uniform active. We'll use positive.
```
m = q.max()
z = (q - m)/beta
z_max = z.max()? Since q-m <=0, max=0. exp_z = np.exp(z - z_max) (z_max may be 0)
Z = exp_z.sum()
weights = exp_z/Z
F = m + beta * math.log(Z)
return F, weights
```
Because z_max=0 if max attained; but if due to nan? no. `exp(-large)` underflows okay.

- `objective_value_smooth(p, beta)`: q, F,w, return F.
- `gradient_p(p, weights)`: g=2*crossconv.

```
idx=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
p = 1.0/np.sqrt(idx + gamma)
p/=p.sum()
return p
```

`p_to_u(p)`: logit = log(p) - log(p[-1]) for first n-1, last 0. Ensure p>0. Add epsilon floor if zeros.
```
p = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
u = np.log(p[:-1])-np.log(p[-1])
```

```
x=np.empty(n); x[:-1]=u; x[-1]=0.0
x-=x.max()
e=np.exp(x)
p=e/e.sum()
```

- p=u_to_p(u)
- q=self_conv(p)
- F,weights=smooth_max_and_grad(q, STATE['beta'])
- grad_p=2*crossconv(weights,p)
- mean = np.dot(p, grad_p)
- grad_u_full = p*(grad_p - mean)
- grad_u = grad_u_full[:-1]
Store `STATE['last_u']=u.copy()`, `STATE['last_F']=F`, `STATE['last_grad']=grad_u`, `STATE['last_p']=p`, maybe `M=q.max()`.
Return F,grad_u,p,q.

`jac_u(u)`: if cache valid: return cached; else compute and return.

```
def optimize_n(n, time_limit, start_us):

       p = u_to_p(res.x); score=...
       if score<best: update
       check time
```

Custom mirror descent details:
State p normalized, positive.
Initialize p_power.
For iter:
- compute q, M=maxq, score=2nM.
- weights = softmax((q-M)/delta) where delta perhaps M/20 (or adaptive). If M small, delta = 0.05*M? Actually for weights, if M ~1/n, delta ~ M/20 ~1/(20n). Weight exponent max0; near peaks. Underflow okay.
- grad_p=2crosscorr(weights,p)
- Update p_new = p * exp(-eta * grad_p / scale) where scale maybe mean(grad_p) or max(grad_p). Since F homogeneous, we can choose eta based on gradient magnitude. A natural step for mirror descent on squared objective: q ≈ M, gradient ∼2p. If we want reduce p_i at high-gradient locations. Let's set `step = eta * p.mean()`? For uniform p, grad near 2p mean 2/n; p*exp(-eta*grad) changes by threshold? We can line search.
- Backtrack over eta in [0.01,0.1,0.3,1,3,10]? Evaluate score for candidate. Accept if score<best. Since computing candidate score requires convolution per eta; can instead evaluate smooth F, but we can just score (max). We can limit to few etas per iter.
Alternatively use multiplicative update with fixed `eta=1` and line search? The score may oscillate. We can also use **exponentiated gradient with learning rate η=1** on F: p_i *= exp(-η grad_i). For F with smooth beta=M/10, grad ~? We can line search on F.

Line search on F with softmax weights:
- F(p) smooth. Update p' = normalize(p * exp(-η grad)). Since log p step. We can choose η to minimize F'. Try geometric list and pick best F (or score). As F decreases, reduce max step.
- Use adaptive delta: after each accepted, update `delta = max(best_M / 20, best_M / 200?)`. Maybe `delta = best_M * 0.1`. Starting delta=best_M.
- To escape local, occasionally add random perturbation: p_i *= exp(σ ξ_i)/normalize with small σ (e.g., 0.01) and accept if improves after? But pure gradient might converge.

Will mirror descent converge to <1.5? It is a first-order method on a relatively flat problem; may be slow but with 1800s and FFT for n=2048 can do thousands iterations. Let's estimate: Each iteration one conv O(n log n) ~0.001s for n=1024? Actually FFT 2048 ~0.0002s; crosscorr another FFT ~0.0002; total 0.0005s. 10k iterations 5s. Score evaluations for line search several each maybe 20k; still <10s. So we can do many. This could be effective and scalable to n=10000 (FFT 32768/65536 ~0.005s * line searches maybe 1s per 100? Actually 100k ops? Let's estimate FFT 65536 double 2 transforms ~0.002s? crosscorr one transform; total 3 ~0.006s; 10k=60s. OK). We can run large n.

Let's use custom mirror descent as main because no scipy dependency and can iterate many. We can also use L-BFGS for small n if available.

We need tune mirror descent. Let's derive a good natural gradient step. At current p, Fβ. The natural gradient on simplex under KL is ∇_natural = (∇F/p). Mirror step p'∝exp(log p - η ∇F) = p exp(-η∇F). If ∇F_i≈2 p_i, step changes log p by -η*2p_i, tiny for p_i~1/n. Need η~n to have O(1) log changes. For example p fractional ~0.001; grad ~0.002; η=1000 changes by factor e^-2. So base η maybe C*n. We can line search over η scaled by n: candidates [0.01*n,0.03*n,0.1*n,0.3*n,1*n,3*n,10*n,30*n]. For n=1024, up to 30000. p*exp(-η grad) might under/overflow; we can compute in log: logp_new = logp - η grad, subtract max, exp. This allows large steps. Normalization.

But large steps may destroy positivity or oscillate. Line search will choose best F.

Pseudo:
```
def mirror_descent(p, time_limit, initial_delta=None):
  p=p.copy(); p/=p.sum(); p=np.maximum(p,1e-12); p/=p.sum()
  logp=np.log(p)
  best_M, best_score = score(p)
  M=best_M
  delta=max(M*0.5, 1e-12)
  for it in range(max_iter):
     q=self_conv(p); M=q.max(); score=2nM
     # delta = M * 0.1? But for weights stable: want exponent range ~10. delta=M/10.
     delta = max(M/10.0, 1e-12)
     z=(q-M)/delta # <=0
     weights=np.exp(z)/np.exp(z).sum() # z max 0
     grad=2*crossconv(weights,p)
     grad_mean=np.dot(p, grad) maybe? Since objective homogeneous, gradient orthogonal? For smooth F not necessarily. We can subtract mean? In log space, adding constant to grad doesn't change p' (normalization cancels). We can subtract grad_mean to improve numerical: grad_centered=grad - grad_mean.
     # Candidate eta list relative to n
     for eta in [0.005,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1,2,5]*n? maybe
         logp_new=logp - eta*(grad - grad.mean())
         logp_new-=logp_new.max()
         p_new=np.exp(np.clip(logp_new,-700,0))
         s=p_new.sum(); p_new/=s
         Fnew = smooth_F? We can compute q_new and Mnew directly or smooth.
         # Evaluate smooth with same delta? Simpler score =2n max(q_new). If better, accept.
```

We can optimize self_conv using FFT with real input. Implement robust:
```
def fft_convolution(a,b):
   n=len(a), m=len(b)
   size=n+m-1
   L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
   fb=np.fft.rfft(b, L)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb, L)
   return c[:size]
```
For self: `fft_convolution(p,p)`.
For cross: `fft_convolution(weights, p[::-1])`.
This creates new arrays; memory O(L). For n=4806, L=16384, fine.
For n maybe 20000, L=65536, memory 1MB, okay. Direct if n<=256? FFT overhead maybe; but not critical. Use FFT always for simplicity? For n small, direct faster. But no issue. Mirror_descent function top-level takes p, n, deadline, rng, initial_best.
Pseudo more concrete:
```
def mirror_descent(p, deadline, verbose=True):
    n=len(p); p=p.copy(); p=np.maximum(p,1e-30); p/=p.sum()
    logp=np.log(p)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_M=conv_max_fft(p); best_score=2*n*best_M
    stagnation=0
    for scale in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01]:
       iter_block = 2000 maybe depending time
       for it in range(iter_block):
          if time.time>deadline: break
          q = self_conv(p); M=q.max()
          if 2*n*M < best_score: update
          delta = max(M*scale, 1e-15)
          z=(q-M)/delta
          # if scale large, z none; weights stable
          expz=np.exp(z) # z max 0
          # if sum expz zeros? no
          w=expz/expz.sum()
          grad = 2.0 * crossconv(w,p)
          # center grad
          grad = grad - np.mean(grad)
          # maybe scale grad to avoid huge logp: divide by std?
          # Candidate etas: 0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0 times n? Maybe include 0.01
          best_cand=None; best_cand_score=best_score
          for factor in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0]:
              eta=factor*n
              lp2=logp - eta*grad
              lp2-=lp2.max(); lp2=np.clip(lp2,-60,0)
              p2=np.exp(lp2); p2/=p2.sum()
              q2=self_conv(p2); M2=q2.max(); s2=2*n*M2
              if s2 < best_cand_score:
                  best_cand=(lp2,p2,s2)
          if best_cand:
              logp,best_p? ; p=best_p_cand; best_score=...; stagnation=0
          else:
              stagnation +=1
              if stagnation > 50:
                  # small random multiplicative perturbation
                  noise=rng.normal(0,0.05,n)
                  logp += noise
                  logp -= logp.mean? (normalization handles)
                  p=np.exp(clip(logp)); p/=p.sum()
                  q=self_conv(p); M=q.max(); if score<best update
                  stagnation=0
              # optionally reduce delta? next scale loop handles
    return p,best_score
```

```
def lp_optimize(p, run_deadline, rng):
  p=normalize; n=len(p); best_score=fft_score(p)
  consecutive_bad=0
  while time<deadline:
     q=self_conv(p); M=...
     if 2nM<best: update
     A=build_A(p)
     res=linprog(...)
     if not success: break/perturb
     y=res.x / sum(res.x)
     best_t=None
     for t in [0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0]:
         c=(1-t)p + t*y
         c=c/c.sum()
         s=fft_score(c)
         if s<best_t: ...
     if candidate improves current (or best): p=c; consecutive_bad=0
     else:
         consecutive_bad +=1
         if consecutive_bad>5: perturb p, maybe reduce M? 
         if consecutive_bad>20: break
  return p,best_score
```

Alternatively, solve the LP using **KKT iteration**: Given p, M, we can maintain y and g:

- Complementary slackness: g_i w_i=0, y_k s_k=0.

Let's implement `build_convolution_A(p)` top-level returning sparse CSR. It uses scipy.sparse; if not, raises.

```
def build_convolution_A(p):
    from scipy.sparse import coo_matrix
    n=len(p); ar=np.arange(n); total=n*n
    rows=np.empty(total, dtype=np.int64)
    cols=np.empty(total, dtype=np.int64)
    vals=np.empty(total, dtype=np.float64)
    idx=0
    for j in range(n):
        rows[idx:idx+n] = j + ar
        cols[idx:idx+n] = j
        vals[idx:idx+n] = p[j]
        idx += n
    A = coo_matrix((vals,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n)).tocsr()
    A.eliminate_zeros()
    return A
```
This loop for n=2048 4M; okay. For n=1024 okay. For n=4096 total16M, memory 256MB; maybe borderline. We'll set LP max n 1024 or 2048 if time.

`c = -np.ones(n)`, `b = M*(1+1e-12)+1e-15`. Bounds `[(0,None)]*n` (list). For Highs, bounds can be `(0.0, None)` tuple repeated? Use `bounds=[(0.0, None)]*n` okay.

`res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': ...?}`. Linprog doesn't have time_limit. If solve hangs, bad. We can only call for n=1024. To avoid hanging, set a small iteration limit? Highs has options? `options={'max_iter': 100000}` maybe not. Could be okay.

If `res.x` None or not success, return None.

```
def central_profile(n):
    a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
    for j in range(1,n): a[j]=a[j-1]*(2*j-1)/(2*j)
```

Now, mirror_descent: use FFT; for n small <=256 use `np.convolve` in `convolution_fast`. But if using FFT for all, line search stable. Let's implement generic with threshold 256 for speed.

```
def convolution_fast(a,b, direct_threshold=256):
    n=len(a); m=len(b)
    if max(n,m) <= direct_threshold and n*m <= 200000:
        return np.convolve(a,b)
    size=n+m-1
    L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,L)[:size]
```
For self, n*m large but use FFT.

Potential issue: For polynomial square, `fa*fb` with fb same as fa; `fa*fa`. But using generic with a,b=p,p works. For cross, w,p[::-1] required, not conjugate. Good.

In `self_conv_fast`, if n<=128 direct else FFT. We can call `convolution_fast(a,a)`.

In `cross_weights_fast`, call `convolution_fast(w, p[::-1])`; slice. But if using direct `np.convolve`, result exact. If FFT, good.

Implement interpolation top-level:
```
def interpolate_profile(p_small, n_big):
    if len(p_big)==len(p_small): return p_small
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,len(p_small))
    y=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_big)
    p=np.interp(y,x,p_small)
    p=np.maximum(p,1e-10); return p/p.sum()
```

```
run_plan.append(('mirror',N_small,'tail',80))
```

```
def exact_score(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
    p=np.clip(p,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(np.sum(p))
    if s<0.01: return float('inf')
    c=np.convolve(p,p)
    mb=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0*len(p)*mb/(s*s))
```

```
def mirror_descent(p, run_deadline, rng):
    n=len(p)
    p=normalize_positive(p)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fft_score(p)
    logp=np.log(p)
    delta_scale=0.15
    stall=0; consecutive_no_best=0
    noise_scale=0.05
    etags=np.array([0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0])
    if n>2000: etags=etags[:6]
    while time.monotonic() < run_deadline - 1.0:
        q=self_conv_fast(p)
        M=float(np.max(q))
        curr=2.0*n*M
        if curr < best_score:
            best_score=curr; best_p=p.copy()
            consecutive_no_best=0; stall=0
        delta=max(M*delta_scale, 1e-18)
        z=(q-M)/delta
        # If z has nan, fallback
        expz=np.exp(z)
        zsum=expz.sum()
        if not np.isfinite(zsum) or zsum <=0:
            w=np.zeros_like(q); w[int(np.argmax(q))]=1.0
        else:
            w=expz/zsum
        grad=2.0*cross_weights_fast(w,p)
        grad=grad - np.mean(grad)
        rms=np.sqrt(np.mean(grad*grad))
        if not np.isfinite(rms) or rms < 1e-15:
            # perturb
            ...
            continue
        grad = grad / rms
        best_cand_p=None; best_cand_log=None; best_cand_score=curr
        for eta in etags:
           lp=logp - float(eta)*grad
           lp -= np.max(lp)
           lp = np.clip(lp, -60.0, 0.0)
           p2=np.exp(lp)
           s2=p2.sum()
           if s2 <=0 or not finite: continue
           p2 /= s2
           q2=self_conv_fast(p2)
           M2=float(np.max(q2))
           sc=2.0*n*M2
           if sc < best_cand_score:
              best_cand_score=sc; best_cand_p=p2; best_cand_log=lp.copy(); best_cand_M=M2
        if best_cand_p is not None and best_cand_score < curr*(1.0 - 1e-10):
           p=best_cand_p; logp=best_cand_log
           stall=0
           # maybe reduce delta if many improvements
           if consecutive_no_best == 0:
               delta_scale=max(0.005, delta_scale*0.9)
           else:
               consecutive_no_best-=1? 
        else:
           stall +=1
           if stall >= 50:
              # random perturbation: block + global
              width=int(rng.integers(max(2, n//50), max(4, n//5)))
              start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
              logp[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0, noise_scale, width)
              logp += rng.normal(0, noise_scale*0.1, n)  # maybe too many
              logp -= logp.max(); logp=np.clip(logp,-60,0)
              p=np.exp(logp); p/=p.sum()
              stall=0
              noise_scale=min(0.15, noise_scale*1.3)
           elif stall >= 20 and best_cand_p is not None and best_cand_score <= curr*(1+1e-4):
              p=best_cand_p; logp=best_cand_log; stall=0
    return best_p, best_score
```

```
    while time<deadline-1:

        if sc<best_score:

    return best_p,best_score
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)^2

Strategy:
- Use the one-sided singular 'central-binomial' profile as a strong baseline.
  This gives a score near pi/2 asymptotically and is much better than uniform.
- Run FFT-accelerated mirror descent on the simplex with weights taken from a
  mini-max over the convolution peaks, with line search in log-space.
- Optionally use a small LP ascent step (for moderate n) inspired by the
  old state of the art.
- Track the best exact sequence and fall back to the baseline if needed.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    import scipy.optimize as _scipy_opt
    import scipy.sparse as _scipy_sparse
    _HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCIPY = False

_DIRECT_SIZE = 256


def next_fft_len(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    return 1 << max(1, (int(max(1, m)) - 1).bit_length())


def convolution_fast(a, b):
    """Linear convolution, using direct for small sizes, FFT otherwise."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0 or b.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if max(a.size, b.size) <= _DIRECT_SIZE and a.size * b.size <= 300000:
        return np.convolve(a, b)
    size = a.size + b.size - 1
    L = next_fft_len(size)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, L)
    return out[:size]


def self_conv_fast(a):
    """Fast a * a."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size <= _DIRECT_SIZE:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    size = 2 * a.size - 1
    L = next_fft_len(size)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    return out[:size]


def cross_weights_pattern(weights, p):
    """
    Return g_i = sum_j weights[i+j] * p_j, with weights length 2n-1.
    This is the unscaled gradient direction for self-convolution peaks.
    """
    n = len(p)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    conv = convolution_fast(weights, p[::-1])
    return conv[n - 1: 2 * n - 1]


def normalize_positive(p, floor=1e-300):
    """Clip to [0,1000], remove NaN/Inf, normalize to sum 1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(p.size, dtype=np.float64) / p.size
    return p / s


def fft_score(p):
    """Score using fast convolution (for search)."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    p = normalize_positive(p)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    q = self_conv_fast(p)
    m = float(np.max(q))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * p.size * m / (s * s))


def exact_score(p):
    """Score with the same direct convolution as evaluate_sequence."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    p = np.clip(np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0), 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    q = np.convolve(p, p)
    m = float(np.max(q))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * p.size * m / (s * s))


def central_profile(n):
    """
    One-sided profile based on coefficients of sqrt(1/(1-x)):
    a_j = binom(2j, j) / 4^j.
    Its square has exactly flat first n coefficients (before normalization).
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for j in range(1, n):
        a[j] = a[j - 1] * ((2 * j - 1) / (2 * j))
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    return a / a.sum()


def power_profile(n, gamma=0.5):
    """Simple one-sided power-law profile."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p = 1.0 / np.sqrt(x + gamma)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-300)
    return p / p.sum()


def make_profile(n, kind, rng):
    """
    Create an initial positive profile.

    kind:
      0: central binomial
      1: power law
      2: noisy central
      3: central with right-tail boost
      4: central mixed with uniform
      5: central with moderate random block noise
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    if kind == 1:
        p = power_profile(n, 0.5)
    else:
        p = central_profile(n)

    if kind == 0:
        return p

    if kind == 1:
        return p

    if kind == 2:
        if rng is None:
            rng = np.random.default_rng(1)
        fac = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08, n))
        q = p * fac
        q = np.maximum(q, 1e-12)
        return q / q.sum()

    if kind == 3:
        x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n - 1)
        boost = 1.0 + 1.0 * (x ** 2) + 0.25 * (x ** 4)
        q = p * boost
        q = np.maximum(q, 1e-12)
        return q / q.sum()

    if kind == 4:
        q = 0.85 * p + 0.15 * (1.0 / n)
        q = np.maximum(q, 1e-12)
        return q / q.sum()

    if kind == 5:
        if rng is None:
            rng = np.random.default_rng(2)
        q = p.copy()
        for _ in range(3):
            width = int(rng.integers(max(2, n // 80), max(4, n // 6)))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width)))
            q[start:start + width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.05, 0.1, width))
        q = np.maximum(q, 1e-12)
        return q / q.sum()

    return p


def interpolate_profile(p_small, n_big):
    """Piecewise-linear resample a profile to a larger length."""
    if n_big <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    p_small = np.asarray(p_small, dtype=np.float64)
    if p_small.size == 0:
        return central_profile(n_big)
    if p_small.size == n_big:
        return normalize_positive(p_small)
    xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p_small.size)
    xb = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_big)
    q = np.interp(xb, xs, p_small)
    q = np.maximum(q, 1e-12)
    return q / q.sum()


def build_convolution_A(p):
    """Sparse matrix A such that A g = p * g."""
    n = len(p)
    total = n * n
    rows = np.empty(total, dtype=np.int64)
    cols = np.empty(total, dtype=np.int64)
    vals = np.empty(total, dtype=np.float64)
    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    idx = 0
    for j in range(n):
        rows[idx:idx + n] = j + ar
        cols[idx:idx + n] = j
        vals[idx:idx + n] = p[j]
        idx += n
    A = _scipy_sparse.coo_matrix((vals, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n))
    A = A.tocsr()
    A.eliminate_zeros()
    return A


def lp_optimize(p, run_deadline, rng, max_iters=40):
    """
    LP-ascent local search for moderate n.
    For fixed p, solve:
        max sum(g)
        s.t. p * g <= M, g >= 0
    and mix p with the normalized LP solution.
    """
    p = normalize_positive(p)
    n = len(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(p)
    bad = 0
    iter_count = 0

    while time.monotonic() < run_deadline - 1.0 and iter_count < max_iters and bad < 12:
        iter_count += 1
        q = self_conv_fast(p)
        M = float(np.max(q))
        curr = 2.0 * n * M
        if curr < best_score:
            best_score = curr
            best_p = p.copy()

        try:
            A = build_convolution_A(p)
            b = M * (1.0 + 1e-10) + 1e-15
            c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
            res = _scipy_opt.linprog(
                c,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True}
            )
        except Exception as e:
            print("LP failed:", type(e).__name__)
            bad += 1
            if bad > 4:
                break
            continue

        if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False) or res.x is None:
            bad += 1
            continue

        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
        V = float(np.sum(g))
        if not np.isfinite(V) or V <= 1.0 + 1e-8:
            bad += 1
            continue

        y = g / V

        best_cand = None
        best_cand_score = curr
        for t in (0.01, 0.03, 0.08, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0):
            q2 = (1.0 - t) * p + t * y
            q2 = normalize_positive(q2)
            s2 = fft_score(q2)
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score = s2
                best_cand = q2

        if best_cand is not None and best_cand_score < curr * (1.0 - 1e-9):
            p = best_cand
            bad = 0
            if best_cand_score < best_score:
                best_score = best_cand_score
                best_p = p.copy()
        else:
            bad += 1
            # small random perturbation in log-weight space
            logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
            logp += rng.normal(0.02, 0.08, n)
            logp -= np.max(logp)
            logp = np.clip(logp, -60.0, 0.0)
            p = np.exp(logp)
            ssum = p.sum()
            if ssum > 0:
                p /= ssum
            else:
                p = central_profile(n)

    return best_p, best_score


def mirror_descent(p, run_deadline, rng):
    """
    FFT-accelerated mirror descent / multiplicative-weights search on the
    simplex log p. We use softmax-normalized weights over convolution peaks.
    """
    p = normalize_positive(p)
    n = len(p)
    if n == 0:
        return p, float("inf")

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(p)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        raise ValueError("bad initial score")

    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    logp -= np.max(logp)

    delta_scale = 0.15
    stall = 0
    noise_scale = 0.05
    no_improve = 0

    if n > 2048:
        etas = np.array([0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0], dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        etas = np.array([0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0], dtype=np.float64)

    while time.monotonic() < run_deadline - 1.0:
        q = self_conv_fast(p)
        q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
        M = float(np.max(q))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break
        curr = 2.0 * n * M
        if curr < best_score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best_score = curr
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_improve = 0

        delta = max(M * delta_scale, 1e-300)
        z = (q - M) / delta
        # z is <= 0; stable softmax
        expz = np.exp(z)
        zsum = float(np.sum(expz))
        if not np.isfinite(zsum) or zsum <= 0.0:
            w = np.zeros_like(q)
            w[int(np.argmax(q))] = 1.0
        else:
            w = expz / zsum

        grad = 2.0 * cross_weights_pattern(w, p)
        grad = np.asarray(grad, dtype=np.float64)
        if grad.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            break

        grad -= np.mean(grad)
        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(grad * grad))
        if not np.isfinite(rms) or rms <= 1e-15:
            # direction vanished; perturb
            logp += rng.normal(0.0, max(0.05, noise_scale), n)
            logp -= np.max(logp)
            logp = np.clip(logp, -60.0, 0.0)
            p = np.exp(logp)
            psum = p.sum()
            if psum > 0:
                p /= psum
            stall += 1
            if stall > 80:
                delta_scale = 0.25
            continue

        grad /= rms

        best_cand_p = None
        best_cand_log = None
        best_cand_score = curr

        for eta in etas:
            lp2 = logp - float(eta) * grad
            lp2 -= np.max(lp2)
            lp2 = np.clip(lp2, -60.0, 0.0)
            p2 = np.exp(lp2)
            psum = float(np.sum(p2))
            if psum <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(psum):
                continue
            p2 /= psum
            q2 = self_conv_fast(p2)
            m2 = float(np.max(q2))
            if not np.isfinite(m2):
                continue
            s2 = 2.0 * n * m2
            if s2 < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score = s2
                best_cand_p = p2
                best_cand_log = lp2.copy()

        if best_cand_p is not None and best_cand_score < curr * (1.0 - 1e-10):
            p = best_cand_p
            logp = best_cand_log
            stall = 0
            # shrink the softmax width slowly when improving
            delta_scale = max(0.004, delta_scale * 0.92)
        else:
            stall += 1
            no_improve += 1

            if stall >= 35 and best_cand_p is not None and best_cand_score <= curr * (1.0 + 1e-4):
                p = best_cand_p
                logp = best_cand_log
                stall = 0
            elif stall >= 70:
                # block + gentle global perturbation
                lo = max(2, n // 80)
                hi = max(lo + 1, n // 6)
                width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
                start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width)))
                logp[start:start + width] += rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, width)
                logp += rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale * 0.05, n)
                logp -= np.max(logp)
                logp = np.clip(logp, -60.0, 0.0)
                p = np.exp(logp)
                psum = p.sum()
                if psum > 0:
                    p /= psum
                stall = 0
                no_improve = 0
                noise_scale = min(0.18, noise_scale * 1.25)
                if no_improve > 500:
                    # periodically broaden the peak weighting again
                    delta_scale = max(delta_scale, 0.20)

    return best_p, best_score


def local_block_search(p, run_deadline, rng):
    """Light local multiplicative block search for final polishing."""
    p = normalize_positive(p)
    n = len(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(p)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return best_p, best_score

    while time.monotonic() < run_deadline - 1.0:
        lo = max(2, n // 160)
        hi = max(lo + 1, n // 8)
        width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width)))

        cand = p.copy()
        noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.12, width)
        cand[start:start + width] *= np.exp(noise)
        cand = np.clip(cand, 1e-15, 1000.0)
        s = cand.sum()
        if s <= 0.01 or not np.isfinite(s):
            continue
        cand /= s
        sc = fft_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_p = cand

    return best_p, best_score


def safe_evaluate(seq):
    """Call the provided evaluator if available, otherwise exact_score."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return exact_score(np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64))


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Search and return the best non-negative sequence found."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0:
        budget = 1800.0
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, budget - 8.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 12345)

    print("starting search, budget_s=", budget)

    # ---- Baselines ----
    base_lengths = [512, 1024, 2048, 4096]
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1
        if isinstance(h, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
            harr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
            if harr.ndim == 1 and harr.size > 0:
                harr = normalize_positive(harr)
                Lh = harr.size
                if 32 <= Lh <= 5000:
                    base_lengths.append(Lh)
    except Exception:
        h = None
        Lh = 0

    base_lengths = sorted(set(int(x) for x in base_lengths if 32 <= int(x) <= 5000))

    best_p = central_profile(max(512, len(base_lengths)))
    best_score = float("inf")
    best_exact = float("inf")
    best_exact_p = None

    for n in base_lengths:
        for kind in (0, 1):
            p = make_profile(n, kind, rng)
            p = normalize_positive(p)
            sc = fft_score(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_p = p.copy()
            scx = exact_score(p)
            if np.isfinite(scx) and scx < best_exact:
                best_exact = scx
                best_exact_p = p.copy()

    print("baseline full-eval best:", best_exact, "at length",
          best_exact_p.size if best_exact_p is not None else 0)

    # Choose primary large length.
    if h is not None and 2048 <= Lh <= 5000:
        N_large = int(Lh)
    else:
        N_large = 4096
    N_medium = min(2048, N_large)
    N_small = min(1024, N_large)

    # ---- Small LP stages (if scipy is available) ----
    stored_small_profile = None
    if _HAVE_SCIPY and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
        for Nlp in (min(1024, N_large), min(512, N_large)):
            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 20:
                p0 = central_profile(Nlp)
                run_dead = min(deadline - 5.0, time.monotonic() + 90.0)
                plp, slp = lp_optimize(p0, run_dead, rng, max_iters=30)
                if np.isfinite(slp):
                    scx = exact_score(plp)
                    if np.isfinite(scx) and scx < best_exact:
                        best_exact = scx
                        best_exact_p = plp.copy()
                        print("LP improved:", scx, "n=", Nlp)
                    if slp < 2.0 and slp < (1.7 if stored_small_profile is None else fft_score(stored_small_profile)):
                        stored_small_profile = plp

    # If we have a good small profile, interpolate it to the large scale.
    interp_profile_large = None
    if stored_small_profile is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
        interp_profile_large = interpolate_profile(stored_small_profile, N_large)

    # ---- Primary mirror search on large length ----
    run_specs = []
    run_specs.append(("kind", N_large, 0))
    run_specs.append(("kind", N_large, 2))
    run_specs.append(("kind", N_large, 3))
    if h is not None and 2048 <= Lh <= 5000:
        run_specs.append(("height", Lh, 0))
    if interp_profile_large is not None:
        run_specs.append(("interp", N_large, 0))
    run_specs.append(("kind", N_medium, 3))
    run_specs.append(("kind", N_small, 2))

    # Larger length gets more time.
    time_weights = {0: 260.0, 1: 180.0, 2: 130.0, 3: 120.0, 4: 110.0, 5: 90.0, 6: 70.0}

    for i, (kind, n, _) in enumerate(run_specs):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 25.0:
            break
        wt = time_weights.get(i, 70.0)
        run_time = min(wt, max(25.0, remaining - 10.0))
        if run_time < 20.0:
            break

        if kind == "height" and h is not None and n == Lh:
            p0 = h.copy()
        elif kind == "interp" and interp_profile_large is not None and n == N_large:
            p0 = interp_profile_large.copy()
        else:
            p0 = make_profile(n, i % 5, rng)

        p0 = normalize_positive(p0)
        if p0.size == 0:
            continue

        run_dead = min(deadline - 3.0, time.monotonic() + run_time)
        try:
            p1, s1 = mirror_descent(p0, run_dead, rng)
        except Exception as e:
            print("mirror descent skipped:", type(e).__name__, e)
            p1, s1 = p0, fft_score(p0)

        if s1 is None or not np.isfinite(s1):
            continue
        if s1 < best_score:
            best_score = s1
            best_p = p1.copy()
        scx = exact_score(p1)
        if np.isfinite(scx) and scx < best_exact:
            best_exact = scx
            best_exact_p = p1.copy()
            print("mirror updated exact best:", scx, "n=", p1.size, "wall=",
                  time.monotonic() - t0)

    # ---- Final polishing on best exact sequence ----
    if best_exact_p is None:
        best_exact_p = central_profile(1024)

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
        ppol, _ = local_block_search(best_exact_p, deadline - 2.0, rng)
        scx = exact_score(ppol)
        if np.isfinite(scx) and scx < best_exact:
            best_exact = scx
            best_exact_p = ppol.copy()

    # ---- Exact final check and fallback ----
    fallback = central_profile(1024)
    seq = [float(x) for x in best_exact_p]
    try:
        final_score = safe_evaluate(seq)
    except Exception:
        final_score = exact_score(best_exact_p)

    fb_score = safe_evaluate([float(x) for x in fallback])
    if not np.isfinite(final_score) or final_score > max(2.5, fb_score):
        seq = [float(x) for x in fallback]
        final_score = fb_score

    # Safety: if two fallback attempts are still bad, return tiny central sequence.
    if not np.isfinite(final_score) or final_score > 5.0:
        tiny = central_profile(256)
        seq = [float(x) for x in tiny]
        try:
            final_score = safe_evaluate(seq)
        except Exception:
            final_score = exact_score(tiny)

    print("final reported score:", final_score, "length:", len(seq))
    return seq
```
